**Book 198:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas II* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-898-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 2 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance plus one guide section per student chapter, with lesson flow, the two-step word-problem method (known → asked → solution → conclusion) and four keyword cards, misconception handling, parent interaction, assessment rubrics, remedial and enrichment tasks, answer keys and reproducible aids (printed pp. 1–184). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Chapter 1 pages (printed pp. 20–42) and Chapter 2 cover (p. 43) misprint the running footer as "Kelas I"; cite the cover and title page. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 199:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas IV* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Hobri, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Haris Setiya Budi, Moh. Iqbal Helmi, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-909-6. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 4 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table with per-topic hour allocations, printed pp. 6–17) plus one guide section per student chapter with objectives, teaching guidance and answer keys (printed pp. 1–252). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Bab 1 objectives list prints items 1.1–1.7 only; items 1.8–1.10 (operations and factors/multiples) stand in its ATP table and chapter scheme — an objectives-list omission, not missing teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 200:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas V* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Meita Fitrianawati, Ika Surtiani, Ait Istiandaru, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-899-1. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 5 student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (printed pp. 1–14, stating the Fase C outcomes, which cover more than this Grade 5 book alone) plus one guide section per student chapter, with full answer keys (a 0–4 scoring rubric per test item), misconception notes, differentiation, remedial and enrichment tasks, parent interaction and reading lists (printed pp. 1–428). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |

| Dividing into equal parts: half — dividing equally (Тэнцүү хуваах): the children folded a rectangular sheet of paper into 2 equal parts — observe the children's folding: who folded correctly (Khuslen's straight half fold; Ider's and Anand's uneven folds)? Check whether the fold is right: cut the paper along the folded part, put the cut parts on top of each other, and check whether they coincide exactly; speech boxes: one part of a rectangle divided into 2 equal parts — its half — is coloured; one part of a shape divided into 4 equal parts — half of the half — is coloured; observe the pictures and answer: a. which shape's half is coloured? b. which shape's half of a half is coloured? (a triangle with half coloured; a rectangle with one of four parts coloured; a pentagon with one of five sectors coloured; a rhombus with three of four triangles coloured; a circle with one quarter coloured); a check-it task: are the equal parts coloured? (two circles, one divided into unequal horizontal strips with one coloured, one divided into quarters with two opposite parts coloured) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 113 |
| Half and quarter of a metre, fraction notation — the girl made a decoration with half of her 1 metre ribbon — find with how many metres of ribbon she made the decoration: to find the ribbon's half, fold the 1 metre ribbon into 2 equal parts; one part of 1 m divided into 2 equal parts is equal to 1/2 m, read "one half of a metre" (хоёрны нэг метр); observe how the ribbon was divided and answer: the ribbon's half was divided again — half of a half; one part of 1 m divided into 4 equal parts is equal to 1/4 m, read "one quarter of a metre" (дөрөвний нэг метр); fill in the number in the box: of the pictured strip, the part coloured blue, the part coloured pink, and the parts coloured green are written as fractions of the whole strip | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 114 |
| Halves and quarters of shapes and quantities — colour the shapes' halves (a rectangle, a triangle, a parallelogram, a 2×2 grid, a circle, a rhombus); colour half of the shapes' halves (a subdivided parallelogram, a long thin rectangle, a column-divided rectangle, a circle in quarters, a T-shaped grid); write the matching number in the box: half of 8 sweets is □ sweets (four boxes of sweets pictured in pairs); half of 6 sweets is □ sweets; a. half of the half of 12 sweets is 3 sweets — check with the picture (12 sweets in 4 boxes of 3); b. half of 8 sweets is □ (8 sweets pictured in 2 rows); Khulan read half of the half of her 20-page book — how many pages of the book did she read?; Boldoo gave half of the 8 apples he had to his younger brother — how many apples were left for Boldoo?; mother gave her elder daughter half of the half of 12 pears — how many pears did she give her daughter?; a fruit table (apples, oranges and bananas pictured in rows): how many of each fruit are there? the half of all the apples is equal to how many? the half of all the oranges? the half of all the bananas? can the half of all the oranges be more than the half of the bananas? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 115 |
| Test yourself (Өөрийгөө сориорой): 1. underline the expression whose difference value does not come to 34 (18 + 16; 51 − 17; 71 − 16; 27 + 7; 93 − 59); 2. write the number of the whole gridded shape in sum form and find its value (an 8 × 5 grid with a coloured part — 25 white cells and 15 pink cells: 25 + 15 = 40); 3. the children strung mushrooms: which product matches the value of the strung mushrooms (a. 8 + 8 + 8; b. 3 + 3 + 3 + 3 + 3 + 3 + 3 + 3; c. 3 × 8; d. 8 × 3)?; 4. read the dialogue and answer the question: the girl asks "What time is it?", the boy answers "The long hand is on 7 and the short hand has passed 2" — what time was it (a. 7 hours 2 minutes; b. 2 hours 7 minutes; c. 2 hours 35 minutes; d. 7 hours 10 minutes)?; 5. find the three-sided, right-angled shape among the pictured shapes and circle its number (a triangle, a square, a triangle, a rhombus, a right triangle); 6. find the shape divided into 4 equal parts and colour it to match the writing 1/4 (an oval with crossing diagonals, a triangle subdivided into 4 small triangles, a circle with vertical strips, a rectangle with a diagonal and a half); 7. a. find the product's value: 26 × 2; b. using the previous product's value, calculate: 56 × 2 | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 152 |
| Fractions from measuring a ribbon; numerator and denominator — getting to know common fractions (Энгийн бутархайтай танилцах) — reading and writing common fractions: a boy cut a ribbon as long as his arm span with scissors: express that ribbon's length in metres: when the ribbon was measured using a metre, it came out more than 1 m; a girl: "How can the part of the ribbon longer than 1 m be expressed in metres? Compare its length with 1 m"; a boy: divide 1 m into 3 equal parts and have a look: one of its parts is equal to the ribbon's extra part; a girl: "One part of 1 m divided into 3 equal parts equals 1/3 m. It is read 'one third of a metre' (гуравны нэг метр)"; the writing order for the fraction 1/3 is shown in a box (the denominator, the fraction line and the numerator written in numbered order); divide a 1 m ribbon into equal parts: the ribbon's coloured part equals how many metres, and the uncoloured part's length is how many metres (three ribbons divided into 2, into 5 and into 4 equal parts with some parts coloured, the fractions to be written in box-over-box form); draw three right angles (squares) representing a ribbon 1 m long: colour the part of the ribbon of the given length (a. 1/4 m; b. 1/8 m; c. 1/10 m); in the following pictures, express the coloured and uncoloured parts' lengths in metres and write them (a ribbon divided into 5 equal parts with 3 parts coloured: each part is 1/5 m, so the coloured part is 3/5 m, written 3/5 and read 'three fifths of a metre'); a girl's speech: like the traditional measures (төө, сөөм), the ribbon's length can be understood as being measured with a unit of one equal part of 1/5 m; a box: numbers of the form 1/4, 1/5, 3/5 are called common fractions (энгийн бутархай): in 3/5, 3 is the numerator (хүртвэр) and 5 is the denominator (хуваарь); write the measure of the coloured part of a 1 m ribbon and name the numerator and denominator of the fraction formed (four ribbons divided into equal parts with coloured parts); how many pieces of 1/8 are there in each of the fractions 3/8, 7/8, 6/8, 4/8, 2/8, 8/8?; similarly, determine how many pieces of 1/6 there are in each of the fractions 5/6, 2/6, 4/6, 3/6, 6/6; a 1 m long ribbon is divided into 5 equal parts (a number line from 0 to 1 (m) is pictured with 1/5 marked): 3 pieces of 1/5 are of what length? 4 pieces of 1/5 are of what length? 5 pieces of 1/5 m are of what length?; a boy's speech: "When a 1 m ribbon is divided into 5 equal parts, one part's length is 1/5 m. 5 pieces of 1/5 m are 5/5 m, that is, a length equal to a 1 m ribbon" — with the box 5/5 = 1 | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed pp. 112–113 |
| Comparing fractions with the same denominator — comparing common fractions (Энгийн бутархайг жиших): a picture shows a ribbon divided into equal parts: a. write the fractions expressing the coloured part's length and the uncoloured part's length: what numbers are the numerator and the denominator?; b. using the fractions in the picture, compare them: what conclusion can be drawn?; c. write the fraction expressing the uncoloured part's length; d. how long are the coloured and uncoloured parts altogether?; a boy's speech: "3 pieces of 1/4 are bigger than 2 pieces of 1/4. That is, 2/4 < 3/4, and 3/4 is bigger than 2/4: 3/4 > 2/4"; read the number marked by a dot on the measuring strip (a. a strip from 0 to 1 (m) divided into eighths with dots above three divisions; b. a strip from 0 to 1 with dots): which of the numbers is bigger?; compare and read (a. 1/3 and 2/3; b. 2/4 and 3/4; c. 3/8 and 7/8; d. 3/8 and 6/8; e. 3/5 and 4/5; f. 2/6 and 4/6; g. 4/4 and 3/4; h. 6/10 and 9/10; i. 6/6 and 1; j. 1 and 7/8); name the fractions' denominators and numerators: write them in increasing order: draw a ribbon, mark the parts expressing the fractions and check (a. 6/8, 2/8, 8/8, 3/8; b. 2/6, 5/6, 1, 4/6; c. 9/10, 1, 7/10, 3/10); find the number that fits the box (5/8 < □/8; 9/10 > □/10; □/5 > 8/5; 3/6 < □/6; □/3 > 1/3; □/4 < 3/4; □/□ = 1); find the pattern and write the next two numbers (a. 1/10, 3/10, 5/10, □, □; b. 8/8, 6/8, 4/8, □, □) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 114 |
| Fraction test and consolidation — test yourself (Сорих): of how many pieces of 1/8 do the following numbers consist (7/8; 3/8; 6/8; 1/8; 1; 2/8; 5/8)?; write the common fraction corresponding to the points indicated by arrows (a number line from 0 to 1 divided into eighths, with arrows at three points); write whichever of the signs >, <, = fits and give an example (2/5 and 4/5; 3/4 and 2/4; 1 and 7/8; 5/5 and 1; 3/3 and 6/6); calculate (4/10 + 3/10; 3/5 + 2/5; 5/6 − 1/6; 1 − 3/8; 1/4 + 3/4; 1 − 3/3); consolidation (Бататгах): write the common fractions and name their numerators and denominators (a. consists of 2 pieces of 1/3; b. consists of 6 pieces of 1/6; c. consists of 5 pieces of 1/6; d. consists of 3 pieces of 1/5; e. consists of 7 pieces of 1/8; f. consists of 5 pieces of 1/4); read and write (a. three fifths; b. nine tenths; c. seven eighths; d. five sixths; e. five tenths; f. four thirds); compare (3/6 and 4/6; 9/10 and 7/10; 3/8 and 5/8; 5/5 and 2/5; 1 and 1/2; 3/4 and 2/4); colour the part of 1 metre that corresponds (five 1 m strips divided into fifths, labelled 1/5 m, 2/5 m, 3/5 m, 4/5 m and 1 m, above a number line from 0 to 1 m): observe the coloured picture and work following the instructions: a. find the number that fits the box on the number line; b. how many 1/5 m are contained in 1 metre?; c. which of 3/5 m and 4/5 m is longer, and by how much? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 117 |
| Making fraction strips by folding — divide a 1 m ribbon into equal parts and answer the questions: a. divide the ribbon into 3 equal parts: one piece of 1/3 m is of what length?; b. divide the ribbon into 10 equal parts: 10 pieces of 1/10 m are of what length?; draw the figures representing a 1 dm ribbon and colour the part of the ribbon of the given length (a. 3/4; b. 5/8; c. 4/10); write in increasing order (a. 7/8, 1/8, 1; and 3/8, 5/8, 4/8; b. 1/6, 6/6, 4/6; and 3/6, 2/6, 5/6; c. 2/5, 4/5, 3/5; and 5/5, 1/5, 6/5); find the number that fits the box (a. 2/5 < □/5; b. □/3 > 1/3; c. 4/10 < □/10; d. □/□ = 1; e. □/□ = □/□); an activity: make fraction measuring strips: 1) make a measuring strip for quarters of a decimetre: a. make a 1 dm ribbon from paper; b. fold the ribbon in half; c. fold it in half once more; d. unfold the ribbon: write the measurements using the fold lines for measuring: a girl asks: "How should the ribbon be folded to divide it into three equal parts?"; 2) make a measuring strip for thirds of a decimetre: a. make a 1 dm ribbon from paper; b. use the lines drawn at equal distances between the notebook's lines to make the strip; c. to divide it into 3 equal parts, place the ribbon across the meeting points of 4 lines: mark where the ribbon's top edge meets the lines, make the divisions and write the measurements; 3) make measuring strips for 1/5 dm, 1/6 dm, 1/8 dm and 1/10 dm: use the strips you made when measuring and when comparing lengths | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 118 |
| Fractions on the number line; more fraction of a quantity — draw the following number line in your notebook (a line marked only with 0 and 1): mark the following parts on this line and write the fractions (a. half of 1; b. 1 part of 1 divided into 3 equal parts; c. 2 parts of 1 divided into 3; d. 3 parts of 1 divided into 3; e. 1 part of 1 divided into 4 equal parts; f. 2 parts of 1 divided into 4; g. 3 parts of 1 divided into 4; h. 4 parts of 1 divided into 4); how much must be added to each of the numbers 4/10, 5/6, 1/10, 1/3, 5/8, 1/5 and 1/4 to make 1?; which part of a 1 dm ribbon — 1/8, 1/2, 1/4, 1/6, 1/5, 1/3 or 1/10 — is the longest? Guess, then check; find the numbers (a. 1/4 of 12; b. 7/10 of 80; c. 5/8 of 48; d. 2/3 of 72; e. 3/5 of 275); which do you choose? Why? Why are the answers of a and b different? (comparing a fraction of one quantity with the same and another fraction of another quantity; e.g. 7/10 of 500₮ against 9/10 of 300₮); of the Earth's globe's whole surface, 7/10 is water and the rest is dry land: with what fraction is the dry land part expressed? Find the difference between the water part and the dry land part; a teacher surveyed the pupils about how they would like to spend the rest day: part of the pupils do sports lessons, part go travelling and the remaining pupils wish to rest: if the class has 40 pupils, how many pupils are there in each group?; in a shop a book costs 5000₮ and a drawing notebook 2500₮: a. the goods were reduced by 1/10 of their price: what did the price of each become (4500₮; 2250₮)?; b. for Children's Day the goods were reduced again by 1/10 of the price: what price did the drawing notebook become (2025₮)?; a boxed task: 1) a rope 1 metre long was divided into 6 equal parts and cut: express the length of 4 parts in metres: what fraction is 7 parts (7/6)?; 2) find the pattern and write the next two numbers: 2/10, 4/10, 6/10, □, □ | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 119 |
| Consolidation: reading and comparing fractions — reading, writing and marking common fractions: of how many pieces of 1/4 do the fractions 2/4, 3/4, 1 and 5/4 consist?; write the suitable common fraction (a. a number line from 0 to 1 divided into 4 parts with a box under each part; b. a number line from 0 to 1 divided into 6 parts with boxes); compare the fractions and explain (5/8 and 7/8; 4/5 and 3/5; 5/6 and 4/6; 7/10 and 9/10); read the fractions and compare them (1/5 and 3/5; 1/3 and 2/3; 2/4 and 1/4; 2/6 and 5/6; 3/8 and 5/8; 7/10 and 5/10); depict the numbers 7/10, 1/10, 6/10, 9/10 and 11/10 as segments on a number line: write them in order from smallest to biggest; of a cake, Tuya ate a 1/8 part, Suvdaa a 3/8 part and Chimgee a 2/8 part: who ate the biggest part, and who the smallest?; mark the fractions 1/8, 2/8, 3/8 and 5/8 on a number line with dots: write them listed from smallest to biggest; find the number that fits the box: how many numbers did you write? Why (a. 1/3 < □/3; b. 7/8 > □/8; c. 1 = □/□; d. 1/10 < □/10)?; rectangles of equal measure are divided into 2, 4 and 6 equal parts: compare their coloured parts and explain: compose similar problems yourselves (a. 3 pieces of 1/4 against 5 pieces of 1/6, shown as coloured strips; b. 1 piece of 1/2 against 4 pieces of 1/6 — fractions with different denominators compared from the pictures) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 139 |
| Comparing fractions: same numerator; fraction word problems — depict the numbers 3/4, 3/6, 3/8 and 3/10 on strips and answer the questions (what number is the fractions' numerator? What number is the denominator? What does the denominator express? A fraction with a bigger denominator differs how from the others?); show the fractions 3/5 and 3/6, 2/10 and 2/8, 3/4 and 1/6 with pictures: compare the fractions and explain (a girl's speech: "3/6 is smaller than 3/5, so it is written 3/6 < 3/5"; a boy's speech: "3/5 is bigger than 3/6, so it is written 3/5 > 3/6" — fractions with the same numerator: the one with the smaller denominator is bigger); which of them is arranged in order from smallest to biggest? How did you know (a. 1/2, 1/6, 1/10; b. 1/10, 1/6, 1/2; c. 1/6, 1/2, 1/10; d. 1/10, 1/2, 1/6)?; a pupil has 500₮: he bought nuts for 2/5 of all his money (a strip of 5 parts with 3 parts coloured shows the 3/5 left): how many tögrög does he have left (300₮)?; Grade 3 has 40 pupils: 1/4 of them live in the dormitory: how many pupils live in the dormitory (10)?; pupil Bayar planned to spend his summer holiday like this: on 3/10 of the holiday's days he goes to the countryside, on part of them he sits in English-language training, and on the remaining part he can rest: if the holiday is 80 days, how many days does Bayar go to the countryside, how many days does he sit in English training, and how many days does he rest? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 140 |
| Chapter 2: reading, writing, comparing common fractions — SECOND CHAPTER (Хоёрдугаар бүлэг) — reading, writing, comparing common fractions (Энгийн бутархайг унших, бичих, жиших): let's refresh our knowledge about the common fraction (a. the bar divided into 4 equal parts with 3 parts shaded: the number of shaded parts 3 is the numerator (Хүртвэр), and the number of equal divisions 4 is the denominator (Хуваарь) — the fraction 3/4; b. the bar divided into eighths with parts shaded, the numerator and denominator boxes to fill); a girl has yellow and blue ribbons: how does the girl express these ribbons' measures in metres (the girl's question: "If the ribbons are not a full 1 m long, can their measures be expressed in metres?"; the boy's answer: "The yellow ribbon is the measure equal to 1 part of a 1 m ribbon divided into 3 equal parts, and the blue ribbon is equal to 1 part of a 1 m ribbon divided into 4 equal parts"; the diagram: the full 1 m ribbon above, the yellow ribbon marked 1/3 m, the blue ribbon's fraction to write; the box: "The measure equal to 1 part of a 1 m ribbon divided into 3 equal parts is denoted 1/3 m"); a. a boy can express the amount of water in a vessel of 1 litre capacity in litres: observe the division scale of the 1-litre vessel divided into 10 equal parts (the pictured vessel filled to 7 parts: "the measure equal to 7 parts of 1 litre of liquid divided into 10 equal parts is □/□ of a litre"); the box: "1 part of 1 litre divided into 10 equal parts is one tenth of a litre, denoted 1/10 l"; b. express the amounts of water in litres (the six vessels A to E filled to different tenths); into how many equal parts is the ribbon divided, and which part of it is shaded (the ten ribbon bars divided into from 2 to 10 equal parts with different numbers of parts shaded, a fraction to write for each; a boy's note with the fully shaded bar: the whole divided into 6 equal parts gives 6/6 = 1); write the number lines' divisions as common fractions (the two lines from 0 to 1 with their tick divisions and a box under each tick); mark the 0 and 1 divisions on the number line: express the following parts as common fractions and mark them with a dot on the number line (half of the unit; 1 part of the unit divided into 3; 3 parts of the unit divided into 5); a cook bakes 2 loaves from 1 kg of flour: how many kilograms of flour go into one loaf (1/2 kg); draw a segment 1 decimetre long in your notebook: mark how many centimetres its 3/10 is: can this measure be expressed in decimetres (3 cm; 3/10 dm); draw a rectangle 6 cm long and 3 times shorter in width, and colour its 2/3 part: find the coloured part's area measure (the whole is 12 cm.kv, so the coloured part is 8 cm.kv); compose a problem from the picture (the disc divided into 8 equal sectors coloured blue and yellow: a. into how many equal parts is the shape divided?; b. which part of it is blue and which part yellow?; c. how many parts of the shape are uncoloured?); a self-check: mark the following fractions on the number line with dots and write them in the order of their positions (two sets of fractions with denominators 5, 10, 4, 8 and 2) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 57–58 |
| Comparing fractions with equal denominators and equal numerators — a boy has a ribbon 1/5 m long and a girl has one 3/5 m long: whose ribbon is longer (the two fraction bars over the number line from 0 to 1; comparison method 1, the girl's: the number on the right-hand side of the number line is the bigger one; comparison method 2, the boy's: of fractions with equal denominators, the one with the bigger numerator is bigger); mark the fractions 3/7 and 5/7 on the number line with dots and compare them; compare the fractions (1/4 and 2/4; 3/6 and 5/6; 2/5 and 3/5; 19/126 and 9/126; 3/3 and 1); paint was put into two vessels of equal measure: one vessel's 2/5 part is red paint and the other vessel's 4/5 part is blue paint: which vessel's painted part is bigger (the two pictured cylinders); write the fractions in increasing order (8/9, 5/9, 1/9, 7/9, 9/9; 1/10, 7/10, 3/10, 10/10, 9/10, 5/10; 3/15, 7/15, 16/15, 4/15, 14/15, 15/15, 8/15 — including the improper 16/15); using the digits 2, 3, 4 and 5, compose and write a fraction bigger than 3/5 (the digits may be repeated); write the number that makes the inequality true (□/4 < 2/4; 3/6 > □/6; □/5 < 3/5; 19/126 < □/126; 3/3 > □/3); an activity: using the number cards together, compose common fractions and play (the two rows of cards 1 to 9 in two colours: a. two children in turn each draw one card from the two coloured packs and compose a fraction; b. compare the composed fractions: whoever composes the bigger fraction scores 1 point); a puzzle: which is bigger, six pieces of 1/2 or four pieces of 1/4 (6 × 1/2 = 3 against 4 × 1/4 = 1) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 59 |
| Mixed numbers: reading, writing, comparing; proper and improper fractions — reading, writing and comparing mixed fractions (Холимог бутархай унших, бичих, жиших): how many metres long is the ribbon (the number line from 0 to 3 m where the ribbon covers 2 whole metres and 3/5 of another: a girl's note: "There is a ribbon 2 3/5 m long"; the box: 2 + 3/5 = 2 3/5, with the labels: the whole (Бүхэл) plus the fraction (Бутархай) makes the mixed fraction (Холимог бутархай); the table: the picture, the number line, the whole 2, the fraction 3/5, and the reading "Хоёр бүхэл тавны гурав" — two whole three-fifths); a. express the amount of water in the vessel in litres (the pictured measures: 2 l 900 ml = □ □/□ l; 1 l 100 ml = □ □/□ l; 2 l 750 ml = □ □/□ l — mixed numbers out of millilitres); b. write the amounts of water in order; c. complete the following conclusion (the box: "When comparing mixed fractions, first the whole part is compared: the one with the bigger whole part is … . If the whole parts are equal, the … part is compared"); write the numbers in mixed-fraction form (1 + 3/4; 2 + 1/6; 4 + 4/7; 9 + 3/10; 5 + 2/3; 8 + 8/9); read the spoken numbers and write them (three whole three-fifths; one whole five-sevenths; one whole one-eighth; five whole three-quarters); write the ribbons' lengths against the number line's scale, measure them and compare them (the three ribbons measured in sixths over the line from 0 to 2; the boxes: numerator smaller than denominator — 5/6 < 1, a proper fraction (зөв бутархай); numerator equal to denominator — 6/6 = 1; numerator greater than denominator — 11/6 > 1, an improper fraction (засагдах бутархай)); a. mark the fraction on the number line with a dot and discuss on which side of the whole it lies; b. pick out the improper fractions and write them (1/10, 5/10, 3/10, 7/10, 12/10, 9/10, 13/10, 10/10); write the positions of the points A, B, C and D as common fractions (the number line from 0 to 3 divided in thirds); mark the numbers 1 1/2, 2 1/2, 3 1/2 and 4 1/2 on the number line with dots; a girl has 1 2/5 m of ribbon and a boy has 2 3/5 m for wrapping a present: if the girl and the boy are each given 2 m of ribbon, will those ribbons be enough to wrap the present — depict it by the plate model and calculate (needed: 1 2/5 + 2 3/5 = 4 m; given: 2 + 2 = 4 m — exactly enough); express the ribbons' measures as common fractions and compare them (the two ribbons laid along the pictured rulers, the blue one and the red one) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 62–63 |
| Converting between mixed numbers and improper fractions — observe how the given ribbon's measure was expressed and explain it (the boy's: 1 1/3 = 4/3, with his note: "A mixed fraction can be transferred to an improper fraction"; the girl's: four ribbons each 1/3 m long make the ribbons' total length 4/3 m, shown by the overlapping 1 m bars); observe the pictures and write the measures as fraction numbers (a. the ribbon bars making two whole metres and a quarter part: □ □/□ m = □/□; b. the litre vessels with a part vessel: □ □/□ l = □/□ l; c. the 2-litre vessel filled part-way: □ □/□ l = □/□ l; d. the green surface: □ □/□ kv.m = □/□); discuss how a mixed fraction is transferred to an improper fraction (a. 3 2/5 = 3 + 2/5 = 5/5 + 5/5 + 5/5 + 2/5 = 17/5, built up from the unit fractions; b. 3 2/5 = (3 × 5 + 2)/5 = 17/5; a boy's note: "A mixed fraction is transferred to an improper fraction by two methods"); transfer the mixed fractions to improper fractions (a. 1 5/8 = 8/8 + 5/8 = □/□; b. 2 5/7 = 7/7 + 7/7 + 5/7 = □/□; then by the short method: 4 5/6 = (□ × □ + □)/□ = □/□; 8 3/5 = □/□; and the set: 1 3/4; 2 7/10; 6 7/8; 3 3/7; 4 1/12; 2 7/9); observe how the given ribbon's measure was expressed and explain it (the girl's: the ribbon of 7 quarters, 7/4 m, is 1 m and 3/4 m long; the boy's: 7/4 = 1 3/4, with his note: "An improper fraction can be transferred to a mixed fraction", shown on the bar of 7 quarter-parts against the two 1 m bars); discuss how the children transferred the improper fraction 7/3 to a mixed fraction (the girl's way: 7/3 = 3/3 + 3/3 + 1/3 = 2 + 1/3 = 2 1/3, grouping the wholes; the boy's way: 7 ÷ 3 = 2 (remainder 1), so 7/3 = 2 1/3, by division with a remainder); a girl divided a 5 m ribbon into two equal parts: what length of ribbon did one part become (each part 2 1/2 m, shown by the split bars of 1 m, 1 m and 1/2 m); transfer the improper fractions to mixed fractions (a. 19/8 = 8/8 + 8/8 + □/8 = □ + □/□ = □ □/□; b. 16 ÷ 5 = □ (remainder □), so 16/5 = □ □/□; c. 29/9 = □ + □/□ = □ □/□; d. 23 ÷ 4 = □ (remainder □), so 23/4 = □ □/□; and the set: 6/5; 9/7; 5/4; 8/3; 7/3; 19/10); express the shaded part as a mixed fraction (a. the circles: one whole blue circle and a quarter circle: 1 + 1/4 = □ □/□; b. the pentagons: two whole red pentagons and a pentagon divided into 5 with parts shaded: □ + □/□ = □ □/□; c. the circles: two whole yellow circles and a circle divided into 8 with 5 parts shaded: □ + □/□ = □ □/□) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 64–65 |
| Part problems: finding the part of a number; unit fractions of measures — problems about parts (Хэсгийн бодлогууд): let's find the number, the measure, that corresponds to a part (Хэсэгт ногдох тоо, хэмжээг олъё): draw a 10 cm segment in your notebook and mark its 3/5: how many centimetres of segment did you mark, and how did you calculate it (10 ÷ 5 × 3 = 6 cm); 3/4 of the class's 32 pupils took part in the chess competition: how many pupils took part in the chess competition (the bar model: 32 ÷ 4 = 8 pupils for the 1/4 part; 8 × 3 = 24 pupils for the 3/4 part); travellers must travel 750 km from city A to city B: they travelled the 2/3 part of their journey's road and rested: find the lengths of the travellers' travelled road and of the road remaining (500 km and 250 km); find the number that corresponds to the part (the flower diagrams: a. the centre 36 with the petals 1/2, 1/3, 1/4, 1/6; b. the centre 42 with the petals 1/6, 5/6, 4/6, 2/6; c. the centre 435 with the petals 1/3, 2/3, 1/5, 3/5); find the measure that corresponds to the given part (a. 1/2 of 10 dm = □ cm; b. 1/3 of 15 cm = □ cm; c. 1/5 of 15 m = □ cm (300 cm)); a. how many metres is each of 1/2 km, 3/4 km, 2/10 km, 4/5 km, 1 7/8 km, 13/10 km?; b. how many minutes is each of 1/4 of an hour, 5/6 of an hour, 4/10 of an hour, 5/12 of an hour, 2 3/4 hours, 3/2 of an hour?; c. how many grams is each of 4/100 kg, 1/4 kg, 3/8 kg, 3 1/2 kg, 4/4 kg, 7/10 kg?; a self-check: 1. draw an 8 cm segment in your notebook and mark its 3/4: how many centimetres of segment did you mark?; 2. a rectangular flower garden is 80 m long and 60 m wide: on 5/8 of this field coniferous trees were planted and on the remaining part fruit trees: on what measure of field were the fruit trees planted (the field is 4800 m.kv, so the fruit trees take 3/8 of it, 1800 m.kv) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 69 |
| Finding the whole from a known part; what part one quantity is of another — let's find the whole number, the whole measure, from the number, the measure, that corresponds to a part (Хэсэгт ногдох тоо хэмжээгээр уг тоо, хэмжээг олох нь): the 1/4 part of a ribbon a boy made is 5 cm long: how many centimetres long a ribbon did the boy make (5 × 4 = 20 cm); the 2/3 part of the class's pupils is 20 girls: how many pupils does the class have (the bar model: 20 ÷ 2 = 10 pupils for the 1/3 part; 3 × 10 = 30 pupils for the 3/3 part; the answer: 30 pupils); a. if a shape's 3/4 is 36 cm.kv, find the shape's whole area measure (the pictured cell grid); b. depict the area measure on the number line; compose problems from the bar models and solve them (a. the 2/5 part is 140 m: the whole is 350 m, and the 5/5 part is … m; b. the 5/6 part is 500 kg: the whole is 600 kg; c. the 3/7 part is 90 l: the whole is 210 l); a city household has 483 small livestock: this is the 2/3 part of all its livestock: find the household's whole number of livestock — see the note: as printed this has no whole-number answer, since 483 ÷ 2 × 3 = 724.5; the number 483 is presumably a misprint for an even number; travellers, while travelling, covered the 3/10 part of their road, that is 120 km: how many kilometres of road did they plan to travel in all (120 ÷ 3 × 10 = 400 km); two brothers had 18000₮: they bought notebooks with 1/3 of their money: how many tögrög were left (12000₮); a self-check: 1. the 2/5 part of a sack of flour is 10 kg: how many kilograms is one sack of flour (25 kg)?; 2. the 1/5 part of a good's whole weight is its 300 g box: find the good's total weight with its box, and depict it by the plate model; let's find what part one number, one measure, is of another number, another measure (Тоо хэмжээ нь нөгөө тоо хэмжээний ямар хэсэг болохыг олох нь): a boy had 1500₮: he bought a notebook with 500₮ of it: with what part of all his money did he buy the notebook (the bar model of the 1500 with the 500 marked; the boy's reasoning: "500 fits into 1500 three times, so it is the 1/3 part of all the money"); the Mongolian state flag's pattern is divided into equal parts: each colour is equal to how many parts (the pictured flag with its red-blue-red bands); 18 children are playing near the school: 9 of them are girls: what part of all the children are the girls (1/2); compose problems fitting the pictures and write them (a. the bar of 3000 with the part 1800 marked — the fraction 3/5; b. the bar of 56000 with the part 32000 marked — the fraction 4/7); a rectangle's length is 18 cm and its width 6 cm: what part of the length is the width (1/3); mother received a salary of 169000₮: she spent stated fractional parts of her salary on the electricity bill and on food: what part of all the money is the tögrög left to mother; a rectangle's perimeter is 30 cm and its length is 10 cm: what part of the length is its width (the width is 5 cm, so 1/2); write the given measure as what part of a metre it is (a. □/□ of 1 m is 5 cm (1/20); b. □/□ of 1 m is 75 cm (3/4); c. □/□ of 1 m is 4 dm (2/5)); observe the picture and answer the questions (the coloured grid square: a. find the square's area measure; b. what part of the whole area measure is the green part?; c. what part is the pink part?; d. what part is the yellow part?; e. what part of the square's area measure are the green and pink parts together?); a self-check: 1. Jargal's family has 180 head of livestock: 30 of them are cattle: what part of all the livestock do they make up (1/6)?; 2. express, as a common fraction, what part one measure is of another measure (a. what part of 5 cm is 1 cm; b. what part of 105 kg is 35 kg; c. what part of 10 m is 2 m; d. what part of 60 l is 15 l) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 70–71 |
| Fractions consolidation: summary tables and a two-variant assessment — let's consolidate our knowledge and make a little book (Мэдлэггээ бататгаж, бяцхан ном хийцгээе): the summary table of the common fraction (Энгийн бутархай): the fraction shown on the number line as 1/6, with the terms numerator (Хүртвэр) and denominator (Хуваарь), and the definition "the number of parts taken / the number of equal parts the whole was divided into = numerator / denominator"; the proper fraction 1/6, where the numerator is smaller than the denominator; the improper fraction 7/6, where the numerator is greater than the denominator; the mixed fraction 1 1/6 = 1 + 1/6, a whole part plus a fraction part; the summary table of the common-fraction actions (Энгийн бутархайн үйлдэл): comparing — with equal denominators the fraction with the bigger numerator is bigger (2/15 < 4/15), and with equal numerators the fraction with the smaller denominator is bigger (3/7 > 3/10); adding and subtracting — with equal denominators the denominator is written as it is and the numerators are added or subtracted (8/9 − 2/9 = 6/9); adding and subtracting mixed numbers — the corresponding whole parts and fraction parts are added or subtracted (3 1/7 + 2 5/7 = 5 6/7); the summary table of the three part problems (Хэсгийн гурван бодлого): finding the number or measure that corresponds to a part (3/7 of 42 pupils are girls: 42 ÷ 7 × 3 = 18 girls); finding the whole measure from the part's number (3/4 of how many metres is 15 m: 15 ÷ 3 × 4 = 20 m); finding what part one number is of another (what part of 1 cm is 3 mm: 3/10); let's consolidate our knowledge and do an assessment (Мэдлэгээ бататгаж, үнэлгээ хийцгээе): choose one of the two variants and work independently: swap with your friend, correct the mistakes and give a mark (you may take the teacher's advice if needed); the assessment procedure (Үнэлэх журам): give yourself a mark for each task — "Children! Marks are not important; correcting your mistakes is what is important": all tasks right 😊; 1-2 tasks wrong 😐; 3 tasks wrong 😞; 4 or more tasks wrong — do the work again; Variant A: 1. find the expression's value (36366 − 17366 ÷ (200 − 162)); 2. a boy read 2/7 of a book on the first day and 3/7 on the second day: what part of the book was not read?; 3. a rectangle's length is 16 cm and its perimeter is 42 cm: find its area measure; 4. Tuya has 3 dresses and 2 accessories: in how many ways can she combine them to wear?; 5. a shop bought flour for 35100₮ on the first day and for 58500₮ on the next day, and on the first day it bought 30 kg less flour than on the second day: what does 1 kg of flour cost (780₮)?; 6. compare (5/6 kg □ 600 g); the self-check: express the measure in the given unit (a. 1/6 of a day in hours; b. 3/10 of 2 m in centimetres; c. 3/20 of one tonne in kilograms; d. 3/5 of 1 kilometre in metres); Variant B: 1. find the expression's value (36323 − 17366 ÷ (200 − 181)); 2. a girl read 3/8 of a book on the first day and 2/8 on the second day: what part of the book was not read?; 3. a rectangle's length is 14 cm and its perimeter is 48 cm: find its area measure; 4. Bat has 2 caps and 3 shirts: in how many ways can he combine them to wear?; 5. a shop sold 200 kg more rice on the second day than on the first: on the first day it sold rice for 351000₮ and on the second day for 585000₮: what does 1 kg of rice cost (1170₮)?; 6. compare (6/25 kg □ 3/25 kg); let's do your own assessment (Өөрийн үнэлгээг хийцгээе): try the tasks by choosing them at your own skill level: the easy level (Энгийн түвшин): 1. convert to the given unit (2/5 kg = □ g; 5 km 250 m = □ m; 250 ml = □ l; 72 hours = □ days); 2. if one cell = 1 cm.kv, find the area measure (the 2 × 4 grid); 3. with the digits 4, 1, 2, how many two-digit numbers can be written (the digits are not repeated)?; 4. what angle does the 3/10 part of a straight angle make (54°, an acute angle)?; the middle level (Дунд түвшин): 1. convert (2/6 of a day = □ hours; 5/20 t = □ kg; 7/10 m = □ cm; 3/1000 l = □ ml); 2. a rectangle is 7 m long and 3 m wide: find its area measure; 3. with the digits 1, 2, 3, how many two-digit numbers can be written (a. the digits are not repeated; b. the digits are repeated)?; 4. the class pupils' test results are shown by a bar diagram (the bar chart of the grades A to F: a. how many pupils took the test in all?; b. how many pupils got the grades A, B, C, D?); the harder level (Хүндэвтэр түвшин): 1. a tourist travelled for 3 days: on the first day 2/5 of the whole road, on the second day 1/5, and on the third day the remaining 18 km: how many kilometres did the tourist travel in 3 days in all (45 km)?; 2. with the digits 0, 2, 3, 4, how many three-digit numbers can be written (a. the digits are not repeated; b. the digits are repeated)?; 3. a pupil bought a book for 15000₮, which was three-fifths (тавны гурван хэсэг) of all his money: how much money did he have at first (25000₮)?; 4. a rectangle's perimeter is 24 cm and its length is 2 times greater than its width: find its sides | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 72–74 |

| Mixed numbers — Mixed numbers introduced from scaling a recipe ×3½: a mixed number = a natural number plus a proper fraction, always greater than one | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed pp. 10–11 |

| Periodic — Generating fraction of a pure periodic decimal by the multiply-and-subtract method: 1,333… gives 9x = 12, so x = 4/3 | Venezuela, Matemática Para La Vida (Grade 7), printed p. 231 |
| Mixed periodic — Generating fraction of a mixed periodic decimal (with anteperiod): 2,14666… gives 900x = 1.932, so x = 161/75; the case 1,999… is posed for discussion | Venezuela, Matemática Para La Vida (Grade 7), printed pp. 232–233 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 55 countries (Kazakhstan, Jordan, South Africa, Egypt, Greece, Armenia, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, Libya, Vietnam, Ghana, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, North Macedonia, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Palestine, Turkey, Tanzania, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Australia, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Bulgaria).** Nepal carries fractions from half and quarter only in Grade 1 (L37, pp. 74–75), through 1/2 to 1/10 with comparing and fraction of a set in Grade 2 English (L18, pp. 61–72) and half/quarter only in the Grade 2 Nepali book (L12, pp. 175–180), numerator/denominator with like-fraction addition and subtraction only in Grade 3 (L22, pp. 89–95), equivalent fractions and like/unlike addition and subtraction with mixed numbers in Grade 4 (L4 §4.1), fraction multiplication in Grade 5 (Unit 12, pp. 77–95), to all four operations on fractions in Grade 6 (पाठ 4, pp. 53–75) and fraction word problems in Grade 7 (Unit 16, pp. 139–143). The Grade 2 English p. 61 label of 'Two fourth' as 2/3 is taught corrected (2/4). Bangladesh carries fractions from halves, quarters and thirds with numerator and denominator named in Class 3 (Ch. 9, pp. 84–98), through proper vs improper fractions, equivalence, reducing to lowest terms by HCF and common denominators by LCM in Class 4 (Ch. 8, pp. 87–102), proper/improper/mixed conversion in Class 5 (Ch. 6, pp. 38–64), comparing, improper/mixed fractions and reciprocals in Class 6 (Ch. 4, pp. 57–102), to GCD and LCM of fractions in Class 7 (Exp. 3, pp. 57–79). Two Class 3 English-only slips on p. 95 are taught corrected: the fraction-subtraction rule swaps minuend and subtrahend (correct: subtract the subtrahend's numerator from the minuend's), and the bubble reading “in 4/5 and 4/5” should read “in 4/5 and 2/5” — the Bangla is correct on both. Iraq carries fractions from the fractions ½ and ¼ in Grade 1 (Ch. 7, stocked by the 2026-11-11 Iraq re-verification sweep merge), unit fractions, 2/3 and 3/4 in Grade 2, through multiples of unit fractions, equivalence, comparing and ordering and adding and subtracting in Grades 3–4, to decimals and fractions with fraction operations in Grades 5–6. Kazakhstan carries the first share on Iran introduces fractions in words in Grade 2, gives the notation, equality and comparison in Grade 3, and develops them in Grade 4.
clock faces and the full Grade 5 fraction course with the
comparing and part-of-a-number rules; Jordan carries the youngest
path (halves and quarters only in Grade 1, then the full first
course in Grade 3 and equivalence with mixed numbers in Grade 4);
Egypt carries the model-first course — first fractions at
Grade 2 Term 2 (equal parts; halves, thirds and quarters;
part of a whole and part of a group), then unit fractions, the
number line, comparing and equivalence in Primary 3 Term 2's
Chapter 9 at its printed folios, and the unit fraction again in
Prep 1's new curriculum (rows completed by the 2026-11-08
re-verification sweep); Greece carries lived sharing
(6 sweets for 3 children, identical chocolate bars) and the
fraction-as-division chapter; Armenia carries the naming lesson
(numerator, denominator) and the coordinate-ray plotting rule;
Sierra Leone carries the picture-first primary path (teacher
plans, no pupil book) and the JSS 1 first course; Mauritania
carries fractions only from Grade 5 — its early grades teach no
fractions at all, stated honestly above; Libya carries parts of a
whole in Grade 2, numerator/denominator and equivalence in Grade
3, and the fraction of a set in Grade 4; Vietnam carries the
"one part out of how many" start and the Grade 4 fraction course;
Ghana carries fractions only as teacher-manual evidence (no pupil
textbook in the set), folding strips and bottle tops; South Africa
carries halves and quarters in Grade 2, fractions to sixths in
Grade 3, and the first definitions of proper, mixed and improper
in Grade 6. Laos carries fractions from their first appearance in Grade 3, through whole–decimal–fraction links and equivalence in Grades 4–5, to rational numbers, repeating decimals and irrationals in Grade 7. Yemen carries fractions from half and quarter in Grade 1, through reading, writing, equivalence and ordering, to improper fractions and mixed numbers and all four operations on fractions. Indonesia carries fractions from halves and quarters in words and pictures in Grade 2, through unit fractions, equivalence and the hundredths-decimal link in Grade 4, comparing, ordering and mixed numbers in Grade 5, fraction-to-decimal conversion in Grade 6, to rational numbers, repeating decimals and irrationals in Grade 7 (Merdeka and K13 editions). Nicaragua carries first fractions in Grade 4 — meaning, fractions of a litre, mixed numbers, conversion and comparing — then equivalent fractions, simplifying and the fraction–decimal link in Grade 5, rationals as a set with order and the number line in the Grade 7 module (module evidence), and a full revision of fraction operations with LCM in the Grade 7 student book. Ethiopia carries fractions from half and quarter in words only in Grade 2 (no fraction symbols), through unit fractions and same-numerator work in Grades 3–4, to proper, improper and mixed fractions in Grade 5, the fraction–decimal–percent link in Grade 6, and rational numbers as a/b in Grades 8–9. South Sudan carries fractions from halves, thirds and quarters as parts of a whole in Grade 2, through equivalent fractions and unit fractions in Grade 3, same-denominator addition and subtraction in Grade 4, fractions with LCM and the fraction–decimal link in Grade 5, decimal/fraction/percent conversions in Grade 6, squares and roots of fractions in Grade 7, fraction–percent and decimal–percent conversions in Grade 8, to the whole fractions course — types, comparing, ordering, all four operations, mixed numbers and recurring decimals — in Secondary 1. Two things are never taught as printed: the Grade 3 chain 1/2 = 2/4 = 4/8 is not printed anywhere in the book, and its Activity 27 claim that 1/4 equals 1/2 is wrong; the Grade 3 teacher guide's Activity 29 key "2/5 = 6/16" is wrong (6/15). Cambodia carries fractions whole from halving in Grade 1 through rational numbers in Grade 8, with the Grade 8 definition taught corrected. Kuwait carries fractions from half and quarter in Grade 1, through half/third/quarter of shapes in Grade 2 and parts of a whole and of a set in Grade 3, to equivalent fractions, simplest form and mixed numbers in Grade 4, number-line fractions in Grade 5, and fraction meaning and equivalence revisited in Grades 6 and 7. North Macedonia enters from its main set — Grade 2 (2023), Grade 3 (2023), Grade 4 (2023), Grade 5 (2022), Grade 6 (2023) and Grade 7 (2025).

China carries fractions whole in Grade 5 Volume 2 — the meaning of a fraction, fractions as division, proper, improper and mixed numbers, the basic property, comparing, and the fraction–decimal interchange.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries fractions from Grade 2's equal parts of a whole to Class 10's rational fractions and partial fractions.

Syria starts fractions in Grade 1 Semester 2 with halves and quarters of shapes, names the unit fractions to eighths in Grade 2, defines numerator and denominator in Grade 3, and works equivalence, comparison and ordering in Grade 4 before proper fractions and mixed numbers in Grade 5. Fractions then become the rational numbers of Grade 7, with the standard form, cross products and GCD reduction in Grades 8–9 Algebra.

Saudi Arabia grows fractions steadily — congruent parts and the half/third/quarter in Grade 1, unit fractions to 1/12 and fractions of a group in Grade 2, numerator and denominator with equivalent fractions in Grade 3, and the full Grade 4 chapter: equivalence, common-denominator comparison, and mixed numbers against improper fractions on the number line — with the older 1445 Term-3 volumes witnessing each Part Two chapter and the partial Grade 4 exercises book adding practice evidence for Ch. 10 only.

Malaysia's Form 1 fraction work is the signed half of its rational-numbers chapter: negative fractions join positive ones on the number line and are ordered by common denominators. (Malaysia's set is secondary-only; the primary fraction build stands on other countries' primary books.)

Rwanda teaches fractions from Year 2 — halves, quarters and eighths by folding, with the numerator, denominator and fraction bar — through Year 3's fractions to denominators of 10, with comparing, ordering, complements to one and fractions of whole numbers in both the English and Kinyarwanda editions, Year 4's mixed fractions and ways of reading fractions aloud, Year 5's equivalent fractions and improper ↔ mixed conversions, and Year 6's fraction ↔ decimal conversion, to Senior 1's rational numbers written as p/q.

Sudan builds fractions in Grade 3 — unit fractions, numerator and denominator, fractions of a set, equivalence, simplifying and comparing — reviews them on the number line in Grade 4, adds mixed numbers in Grade 5 and revisits equivalence among the rationals in Grade 7.

India carries fractions through its NCERT ladder — Math-Magic Class 4–5 and Mathematics Class 6–7 — including half of a whole; fractions of money; meaning; review and comparing.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик I, II, III, IV, V, VI, VII, VIII (Grades 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8): fractions from the first halving of shapes and metres, through numerator and denominator met by measuring a ribbon, to mixed numbers, conversions, and finding the whole from a known part.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Aventuras de Patacalientes* (Grade 3), *Contando con los recursos* (Grade 4), *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5), *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6) and *Matemática Para La Vida* (Grade 7). From its books: from Grade 3: what a fraction is — a cake recipe introduces la unidad, la fracción, numerador and denominador: the denominator says into how many equal parts the unit is split, the numerator how many are taken; from Grade 5: mixed numbers — Mixed numbers introduced from scaling a recipe ×3½: a mixed number = a natural number plus a proper fraction, always greater than one; from Grade 7: simplifying — Simplifying a fraction by dividing numerator and denominator by their GCD: 25/10 = 5/2 and 26/10 = 13/5; simplification replaces a fraction by an equivalent one.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 4: fractions: numerator, denominator and the fraction bar (Lesson 32); comparing fractions that have the same denominator (Lesson 33); from Grade 6: common fractions: proper and improper fractions, mixed numbers; changing mixed numbers to improper fractions and back; making fractions like — finding common denominators; simplifying fractions; comparing and ordering fractions; from Grade 7: rational numbers: p/q form, opposites, rationals on the number line; comparing and ordering rational numbers; chapter summary and exercises (rational numbers).

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 1 (teacher's book): Doubles and halves: know doubles at least to 5, double every one-digit number, half of even numbers to 10,…. from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Forming fractions; types of fractions (proper/improper); Forming equivalent fractions; Mixed numbers and fractions greater than 1; converting between them; Fractions and division (fraction as quotient; finding a part of a number); fraction problems. from Grade 6 (teacher's book): : equivalent fractions; fractions on the number line (plans 13–14); : mixed numbers; fractions greater than 1; improper fraction ↔ mixed number (17/8 = 2 1/8); fraction as…. from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .3 Fractions, decimal numbers and reciprocals (të anasjellët) (plan 66); .5 Operations with mixed numbers (plan 68).

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 7: Los números racionales; Representación gráfica de racionales; Fracciones equivalentes y simplificación; Fracciones propias, impropias y mixtas; Problemas con racionales; from Grade 8: Los números racionales; Dimensiones combinadas; from the Compendium's Secundaria 1 section (Grade 7): Números racionales; from the Compendium's Secundaria 2 section (Grade 8): Los números racionales y sus aplicaciones.

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Equal parts (shares) of a unit: half, third, quarter, fifth…; Common fractions: numerator and denominator; Comparing fractions with equal denominators, on the coordinate ray; Proper and improper fractions; Converting an improper fraction to a mixed number and back; Combined operations mixing common fractions and decimals; from Grade 6: The fundamental property of fractions, derived from the quotient property a:b =…; Reducing (cancelling) fractions; Bringing fractions to a common denominator; Comparing fractions with unlike denominators; Finding a fraction (part) of a number; Finding a number from a known value of its fraction (part); Historical note: fractions in Greece (Heron, Diophantus), India, Nasavi, al-Khwarizmi.

Palestine enters through the pupil's books of its Ministry of Education (Curriculum Centre, Ramallah), all in Arabic — Mathematics Grade 1 in two parts (both fourth edition, 2020), Grade 10 in two parts (Part One third edition, 2020; Part Two second edition, 2019 — the held parts are different editions and are cited as such), and the Grade 12 Technological-stream book (التكنولوجي, first edition, 2019 — the book's own name for its stream, never "Information Technology"). No ISBN is printed in any of the five books. From its books: from Grade 1 Part Two: the half: a whole split into two equal parts, one part is a half; shade a half and write ١/٢; the quarter: a whole split into four equal parts, one part is a quarter; shade a quarter and write ١/٤; choose the fraction (½ or ¼) shown by the coloured part of a shape; unit review: shade a half, shade a quarter, choose the fraction for a coloured part.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 1 syllabus requires the concept of a fraction as part of a whole (the families 1/2, 1/4, 1/8, 1/16; 1/3, 1/6, 1/12; 1/5, 1/10), written symbols with numerator and denominator, combining parts to make a whole, fractional parts of a set, and comparing fractions; Class 2 adds equivalent fractions and expressing fractions in their lowest terms; Class 3 adds mixed numbers and improper fractions and converting between them; Class 4 keeps the concept and the written symbols in the scope and sequence. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 3: whole, half and quarter models built and drawn on squared paper with different objects and shapes, compared with classmates' models (recall); match pictures to the fraction cards whole / half / quarter and name each picture's part–whole state; a breakfast story: a whole egg, a tomato cut into 2 equal parts, a loaf into 4 equal parts — how much of each is on the plate (whole, half, quarter in words); fraction notation introduced on whole/half/quarter: 1/1, 1/2, 1/4 with the fraction bar, numerator as parts taken and denominator as total parts; how each is read ("one in two", "one in four"); table work: for pictured shapes/objects name whole-half-quarter, write the fraction notation and its reading; write the fraction notation of pictured wholes, halves and quarters; model 1/2 and 1/4 in a blank space; match notations 1/4, 1/2, 1/1 to models; what fraction of the whole is the coloured part? (pink, blue, purple models with different part counts); a whole shown as the sum of its equal parts: a watermelon in 8 eighths, a pumpkin in 4 quarters, each part written 1/8 or 1/4 and the parts making 1 whole; split shapes into 5, 3 and 6 equal parts and label every part; unit-fraction activity: draw different wholes on squared paper, split each into a different number of equal parts, colour one part and write its fraction beside it; unit fraction defined: each equal part of a whole is its unit fraction; unit fractions of shapes split into 6 and 5; unit fractions 1/2, 1/3, 1/4, 1/6 shown on the 0–1 number line; match unit-fraction models to 1/9, 1/8, 1/5, 1/4, 1/6, 1/10; write the unit fraction of given fraction models; draw a model and label every part with its unit fraction; numerator and denominator from a story: a cake in 6 equal parts and a pastry in 8 — what fraction of each did each child take (2/6, 1/6, 1/8, 3/8); coloured parts as a fraction: 2 of 3 equal parts coloured is 2/3; 5 of 8 to complete; 2/3 and 5/6 placed on the 0–1 number line by splitting it into that many equal parts; show 3/4, 5/8, 3/8, 4/6 and 2/4 on number lines and models: the denominator says into how many equal parts 0–1 is split, the numerator how many are taken; numerator (pay) and denominator (payda) named on a 3/4 model: denominator = total equal parts, numerator = coloured parts, read "three in four"; the 1/6, 2/6 … 6/6 family built one part at a time; complete a table from models: equal parts, coloured parts, numerator, denominator, fraction notation and its reading (3/4 worked); unit fraction of a modelled fraction: colour 3 of 10 wardrobe doors and model the unit fraction 1/10; 5 of 100 tiles coloured, unit fraction 1/100; a guest is served 3 pieces of a baklava tray cut into 100 equal pieces: model it, write the fraction 3/100, its unit fraction 1/100 and how the unit fraction is read; a garden split into 10 equal parts with 1 planted (1/10) and a jigsaw split into 100 equal parts with 1 piece placed (1/100): every part's notation and the unit fraction; for given models write the fraction, its reading, its unit fraction and the unit fraction's reading; write readings of 3/7 and 4/10; write fractions from readings ("two in nine", "five in eight", "five in eleven", "four in seven"); draw models of 3/5 and 4/8 on squared paper; a true/false path puzzle on numerator, denominator and unit fraction statements (e.g. is 2/5's numerator 5? is its unit fraction 1/5?); performance task: make a poster of fraction models — draw circles/squares/rectangles, cut them out, split into equal parts, colour some, write the fraction beside each model, present it; from Grade 6: a fraction as a division: readiness problems 7 litres shared by 5 cows and 12 kg shared by 15 friends pose a ÷ b before the rule is named; history: Egyptian unit fractions in the Rhind Papyrus — 2/7 written as 1/4 + 1/28 (verified) — as the opener of the operations problem section; from Grade 7: Numbers between integers: a 1-unit interval walked in 2 equal steps, then 4 equal steps, gives non-integer step lengths; Equivalent fractions from equal shares of the same whole (3/6, 2/4, 1/2 of a water allowance name the same amount); Equivalent fractions lie at the same point on the number line; simplifying to coprime numerator and denominator gives the simplest form; Locating rationals given as subdivided number-line points and writing two different rationals for the same point; Recognising equal rationals in different forms and reducing each group to its simplest form on the number line; Four-step method for placing a rational on the number line: sign, bounding integers, divide the interval into denominator-many parts, count numerator-many parts; Position of the minus sign (numerator, denominator or in front of the fraction) does not change a negative rational's value.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: revision of adding and subtracting fractions with the same denominator; proper, improper and mixed fractions; equivalent fractions by multiplying numerator and denominator by the same number; fraction word problems.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Mathematics 4 (student); Mathematics 5 (student): part and whole; comparing parts; the fraction: numerator, denominator; comparing fractions with equal denominators / equal numerators; simple problems: finding a part of a number, or the number from its part; parts and fractions; finding a part of a number; the fraction bar as division; finding the whole by dividing a fraction.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 16 teacher-evidence items from 13 of the guides (Book 6, Book 7, Book 10, Book 19, Book 20, Book 22, Book 23, Book 24, Book 26, Book 27, Book 28, Book 29, Book 30; Grades 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8). The fractions reach in the guides runs from presentation in Grade 3 through the four operations in Grades 5–6.

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 9 items (6 from student books, 3 teacher evidence) from Book S5, Book T4, Book S6, Book T5, Book S7, Book T6.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

Into this lesson the placements stock 14 items (12 from pupil books, 2 teacher evidence) from Book S6, Book S7, Book S11, Book S12, Book S15, Book S17, Book S19, Book S20, Book T36, Book T49.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 32 items (10 from pupil books, 22 teacher evidence) from Book S1, Book S5, Book S9, Book S11, Book S12, Book S13, Book S15, Book S17, Book T1, Book T2, Book T3, Book T4, Book T5, Book T6, Book T8, Book T9, Book T10, Book T11, Book T12, Book T13, Book T14, Book T15, Book T16, Book T17, Book T18, Book T36, Book T37, Book T38, Book T39, Book T40.
